To execute this function, a command for the FPGA must be placed in the sequencer buffer. \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a3435cb10e7c31ef375bf23872986c9c4}{Sequencer\+Calc\+AD9854\+Frequency\+Tuning\+Word}} (const unsigned int \&Sequencer, uint64\+\_\+t ftw0, uint8\+\_\+t bit\+\_\+shift)
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em To use a DDS like a VCO\+: calculates the frequency tuning word for a AD9854 DDS using a centre frequency and a detuning proportional to a ADC value. The frequency tuning word is proportional to the period of the DDS output frequency, i.\+e. it\textquotesingle{}s 1/frequency As with all Analog Devices DDS devices, the value of the frequency tuning word is determined by FTW = (Desired Output Frequency × 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}N)/\+SYSCLK where\+: N is the phase accumulator resolution (48 bits in this instance). Desired Output Frequency is expressed in hertz. FTW (frequency tuning word) is a decimal number. The desired frequency is f = f0 + c \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage = f0 + deltaf voltage is the 16-\/bit value provided by the ADC We don\textquotesingle{}t want to use a multiplication. Instead we approximate, using epsilon = deltaf/f0 \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} 1. delta f = c\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage ftw = 1/f = 1/(f0 + deltaf) = 1/ (f0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} (1 + deltaf/f0)) = (1/f0)\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}(1/(1+epsilon)) \texorpdfstring{$\sim$}{\string~} ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} (1-\/epsilon) = ftw0 -\/ ftw0\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}deltaf/f0 = ftw0 -\/ ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} c \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage = ftw0 -\/ scale \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} ADC\+\_\+value We replace the multiplication by a bitshift, i.\+e. we allow scale = 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}n with n=\mbox{[}0...32\mbox{]}. \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_ae7807aa91bd081ff8dd83522fbddff94}{Sequencer\+Switch\+Debug\+LED}} (const unsigned int \&Sequencer, unsigned int On\+Off)

\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em Switches the sequencer to ignore TCP/\+IP commands. This is useful to prevent the sequencer from being interrupted by TCP/\+IP commands while executing a timing critical task, such as transferring input data from the FPGA BRAM to the DDR. This can be useful if lots of data is acquired at a high rate. Lot\textquotesingle{}s of meaning\+: more than the size of the BRAM input buffer. How much that is depends how you configured the Vivado project that generates the FPGA sequencer firmware. \end{DoxyCompactList}\item

\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em This function sets a register for a device on the sequencer. What this means depends on the device type. This function gives us an easy way to add new functionality to a device without having to programm new DLL functions. Set\+Register maps to the register map given in the device\textquotesingle{}s datasheet. Note\+: this is not currently implemented in the API. \end{DoxyCompactList}\item 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a5f8a2790ef4d7b1539b711a4964a6b10}{Set\+Value}} (const unsigned int \&Sequencer, const unsigned int \&Address, const unsigned int \&Sub\+Address, const uint8\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}Data, const unsigned long \&Data\+Length\+\_\+in\+\_\+bit, const uint8\+\_\+t \&Start\+Bit)
\begin{DoxyCompactList}\small\item\em This function sets a value for a device on the sequencer. What this means depends on the device type. This function gives us an easy way to add new functionality to a device without having to programm new DLL functions. In contrast to Set\+Register, Set\+Value can execute more complex operations, such as calculating a DDS frequency tuning word from the given frequency and then programming that. There can be several Set\+Value functions for the same Set\+Register function. There can be Set\+Value functions that set some parameter, or that trigger some action, not even necessarily related to the registers of the device. \end{DoxyCompactList}\item

\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Create (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{bool}]{Initialize\+Afx}{, }\item[{bool}]{Initialize\+Afx\+Socket}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



Threading model. 

The Control\+Light API is intended to be used from one client thread at a time. Concurrent calls into the same API instance are unsupported, including calls made while another thread is waiting for sequence completion or communicating with the FPGA. Client code must serialize API access externally. The API may report an error or behave unpredictably if it is entered concurrently.

Return convention.

Functions returning ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE return true on success and false on failure in the C API build. In the Python/\+C++ exception build, ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE is void and failures are reported by throwing CLA\+\_\+\+Exception. Functions returning void perform the requested action and report only through the API error state where noted. Functions returning const char\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} return pointers owned by the API; callers must copy the string if they need it after the next API call.

Initializes the Control\+API. If you use a bare function C API, then this function must be called before using any other functions in the API. If you didn\textquotesingle{}t load MFC yet (which is the case if you use Qt), then set 
\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Initialize\+Afx} & to true to initialize MFC core \\
\hline
{\em Initialize\+Afx\+Socket} & to true to initialize MFC socket layer The class wrapped version of this API calls this function in its constructor. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
See return convention above. 
\end{DoxyReturn}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{SequencerCalcAD9854FrequencyTuningWord()}{SequencerCalcAD9854FrequencyTuningWord()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a3435cb10e7c31ef375bf23872986c9c4} 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Sequencer\+Calc\+AD9854\+Frequency\+Tuning\+Word (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{uint64\+\_\+t}]{ftw0}{, }\item[{uint8\+\_\+t}]{bit\+\_\+shift}{ = {\ttfamily 22}}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



To use a DDS like a VCO\+: calculates the frequency tuning word for a AD9854 DDS using a centre frequency and a detuning proportional to a ADC value. The frequency tuning word is proportional to the period of the DDS output frequency, i.\+e. it\textquotesingle{}s 1/frequency As with all Analog Devices DDS devices, the value of the frequency tuning word is determined by FTW = (Desired Output Frequency × 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}N)/\+SYSCLK where\+: N is the phase accumulator resolution (48 bits in this instance). Desired Output Frequency is expressed in hertz. FTW (frequency tuning word) is a decimal number. The desired frequency is f = f0 + c \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage = f0 + deltaf voltage is the 16-\/bit value provided by the ADC We don\textquotesingle{}t want to use a multiplication. Instead we approximate, using epsilon = deltaf/f0 \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} 1. delta f = c\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage ftw = 1/f = 1/(f0 + deltaf) = 1/ (f0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} (1 + deltaf/f0)) = (1/f0)\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}(1/(1+epsilon)) \texorpdfstring{$\sim$}{\string~} ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} (1-\/epsilon) = ftw0 -\/ ftw0\texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}deltaf/f0 = ftw0 -\/ ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} ftw0 \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} c \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} voltage = ftw0 -\/ scale \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*} ADC\+\_\+value We replace the multiplication by a bitshift, i.\+e. we allow scale = 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}n with n=\mbox{[}0...32\mbox{]}. 

Assuming the ADC provides 16 bits min frequency change\+: (1 \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} bit\+\_\+shift) SYSCLK / (2\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}48) frequency range\+: (1 \texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<} (bit\+\_\+shift+16)) SYSCLK / (2\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}48) 2\texorpdfstring{$^\wedge$}{\string^}48= 281,474,976,710,656 SYSCLCK 80000000 bitshift 1\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}\texorpdfstring{$<$}{<}bitshift deltaf\+\_\+min deltaf\+\_\+max 0 1 2.\+84217E-\/07 0.\+018626451 1 2 5.\+68434E-\/07 0.\+037252903 2 4 1.\+13687E-\/06 0.\+074505806 3 8 2.\+27374E-\/06 0.\+149011612 4 16 4.\+54747E-\/06 0.\+298023224 5 32 9.\+09495E-\/06 0.\+596046448 6 64 1.\+81899E-\/05 1.\+192092896 7 128 3.\+63798E-\/05 2.\+384185791 8 256 7.\+27596E-\/05 4.\+768371582 9 512 0.\+000145519 9.\+536743164 10 1024 0.\+000291038 19.\+07348633 11 2048 0.\+000582077 38.\+14697266 12 4096 0.\+001164153 76.\+29394531 13 8192 0.\+002328306 152.\+5878906 14 16384 0.\+004656613 305.\+1757813 15 32768 0.\+009313226 610.\+3515625 16 65536 0.\+018626451 1220.\+703125 17 131072 0.\+037252903 2441.\+40625 18 262144 0.\+074505806 4882.\+8125 19 524288 0.\+149011612 9765.\+625 20 1048576 0.\+298023224 19531.\+25 21 2097152 0.\+596046448 39062.\+5 22 4194304 1.\+192092896 78125 23 8388608 2.\+384185791 156250 24 16777216 4.\+768371582 312500 25 33554432 9.\+536743164 625000 26 67108864 19.\+07348633 1250000 27 134217728 38.\+14697266 2500000 28 268435456 76.\+29394531 5000000 29 536870912 152.\+5878906 10000000 30 1073741824 305.\+1757813 20000000 31 2147483648 610.\+3515625 40000000


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em ftw0} & the centre frequency tuning word. \\
\hline
{\em bit\+\_\+shift} & the bit shift to use. Default is 22, which corresponds to a maximum tuning range of 78k\+Hz and a change of 1.\+19\+Hz per ADC value increment. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\Hypertarget{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a72f33e9b346a9b89489678dca5e7908f}\index{ControlAPI.cpp@{ControlAPI.cpp}!SequencerIgnoreTCPIP@{SequencerIgnoreTCPIP}}
\index{SequencerIgnoreTCPIP@{SequencerIgnoreTCPIP}!ControlAPI.cpp@{ControlAPI.cpp}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{SequencerIgnoreTCPIP()}{SequencerIgnoreTCPIP()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a72f33e9b346a9b89489678dca5e7908f} 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Sequencer\+Ignore\+TCPIP (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{bool}]{On\+Off}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



Switches the sequencer to ignore TCP/\+IP commands. This is useful to prevent the sequencer from being interrupted by TCP/\+IP commands while executing a timing critical task, such as transferring input data from the FPGA BRAM to the DDR. This can be useful if lots of data is acquired at a high rate. Lot\textquotesingle{}s of meaning\+: more than the size of the BRAM input buffer. How much that is depends how you configured the Vivado project that generates the FPGA sequencer firmware. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em On\+Off} & true to make the sequencer ignore TCP/\+IP commands, false to resume normal TCP/\+IP command handling. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
See return convention above. 
\end{DoxyReturn}

Loads SPI timing parameters into the sequencer. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em SPI\+\_\+delay\+\_\+\+CS\+\_\+low\+\_\+start\+\_\+wait} & delay after chip-\/select goes low before writing starts, in sequencer timing units, i.\+e. 10ns for the sequencer internal 100MHz clock. \\

\index{SequencerStartAnalogInAcquisition@{SequencerStartAnalogInAcquisition}!ControlAPI.cpp@{ControlAPI.cpp}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{SequencerStartAnalogInAcquisition()}{SequencerStartAnalogInAcquisition()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_abc2074b1daacdb7e5da7c917f64ed688} 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Sequencer\+Start\+Analog\+In\+Acquisition (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \&}]{analog\+\_\+in\+\_\+type}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \&}]{SPI\+\_\+\+CS}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \&}]{Channel\+Number}{, }\item[{const uint32\+\_\+t \&}]{Number\+Of\+Data\+Points}{, }\item[{const double \&}]{Delay\+Between\+Data\+Points\+\_\+in\+\_\+ms}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



Start analog acquisition on the specified channel. This function places a commandto for the FPGA in the sequencer buffer. The analog in acquisition will start when this command is executed by the FPGA. Only one analog in acquisition can hapen at each moment. A new one can start right after the previous one is finished. The data will be returned at the end of a sequence when using CLA\+\_\+\+Wait\+Till\+End\+Of\+Sequence\+Then\+Get\+Input\+Data. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em analog\+\_\+in\+\_\+type} & Analog in board type. 0\+: AQu\+RA MCP3208 analog in board; 1\+: MCP3208 12-\/bit ADC on Serial\+Port\+Board; 2\+: ADS1256 24-\/bit ADC. \\
\hline
{\em SPI\+\_\+\+CS} & SPI chip-\/select setting to use. This is the 3-\/bit CS bit-\/pattern on the rack backplane. A 3-\/to-\/8 demultiplexer is used on the serial port board to create 8 CS lines. If a board only needs 3 or less CS lines, these lines can be used directly (in that case\+: make sure to only pull one line low at a time). \\
\hline
{\em Channel\+Number} & the channel number to use. \\
\hline
{\em Number\+Of\+Data\+Points} & the number of data points to acquire. \\
\hline
{\em Delay\+Between\+Data\+Points\+\_\+in\+\_\+ms} & the delay between data points in ms. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em I2\+C\+\_\+port} & I2C port index on the sequencer. 1 is the I2C port exposed by the serial port board and used by external hardware. 0 is the I2C port connected to the backplane multiplexer, reading out board EEPROMS and addresses. \\

\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Sequencer\+Transmit\+SPI (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{uint8\+\_\+t}]{chip\+\_\+select}{, }\item[{uint16\+\_\+t}]{number\+\_\+of\+\_\+bits\+\_\+out}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{data\+\_\+out}{, }\item[{uint8\+\_\+t}]{number\+\_\+of\+\_\+bits\+\_\+in}{, }\item[{bool}]{start\+\_\+now}{}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



Writes an SPI transmit command to the sequencer. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. (SPI port 1 will be used, which is the port used by existing control hardware and exposed by the serial port board to the user.) \\
\hline
{\em chip\+\_\+select} & SPI chip-\/select line or encoded chip-\/select value. This is the 3-\/bit CS bit-\/pattern on the rack backplane. A 3-\/to-\/8 demultiplexer is used on the serial port board to create 8 CS lines. If a board only needs 3 or less CS lines, these lines can be used directly (in that case\+: make sure to only pull one line low at a time). \\
\hline
{\em number\+\_\+of\+\_\+bits\+\_\+out} & number of bits to transmit from data\+\_\+out. \\
\hline
{\em data\+\_\+out} & bit payload to transmit. The buffer must contain enough bytes for number\+\_\+of\+\_\+bits\+\_\+out. \\
\hline
{\em number\+\_\+of\+\_\+bits\+\_\+in} & number of bits to read back after the write phase. \\
\hline
{\em start\+\_\+now} & true to start the SPI transfer immediately when the command is executed; false to configure it for Sequencer\+Repeated\+Out\+In without immediate start. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Sequencer\+Write\+Input\+Memory (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{unsigned long}]{input\+\_\+buf\+\_\+mem\+\_\+data}{, }\item[{bool}]{write\+\_\+next\+\_\+address}{ = {\ttfamily 1}, }\item[{unsigned long}]{input\+\_\+buf\+\_\+mem\+\_\+address}{ = {\ttfamily 0}}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



Writes a value to the input memory of the sequencer. This is useful to mark the start or end of a data acquisition, or to mark the type of experimental run in the full fledged version of the Control\+API, which can cycle sequences automatically in the background. To execute this function, a command for the FPGA must be placed in the sequencer buffer. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em input\+\_\+buf\+\_\+mem\+\_\+data} & the data to write to the input memory. \\
\hline
{\em write\+\_\+next\+\_\+address} & true to write the next address, false to write the address given. \\
\hline
{\em input\+\_\+buf\+\_\+mem\+\_\+address} & the address to write to, if write\+\_\+next\+\_\+address is false. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}

This function sets a register for a device on the sequencer. What this means depends on the device type. This function gives us an easy way to add new functionality to a device without having to programm new DLL functions. Set\+Register maps to the register map given in the device\textquotesingle{}s datasheet. Note\+: this is not currently implemented in the API. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline

\hline
{\em Data} & the data to set. \\
\hline
{\em Data\+Length\+\_\+in\+\_\+bit} & the length of the data in bits. \\
\hline
{\em Start\+Bit} & the start bit of the data to set. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
See return convention above. 
\end{DoxyReturn}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{SetValue()}{SetValue()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \label{_control_a_p_i_8cpp_a5f8a2790ef4d7b1539b711a4964a6b10} 
\mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_a5e3652cae8b48c1ad174f0b06bbd06d0}{API\+\_\+\+EXPORT}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_aeb04e3165c1e12a3653ac311ee466995}{ERROR\+\_\+\+CODE\+\_\+\+TYPE}} \mbox{\hyperlink{_control_a_p_i_8h_ae89d33bd59c5669016e4694ae249bd0c}{CLA\+\_\+\+FNDEF}} Set\+Value (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sequencer}{, }\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Address}{, }\item[{const unsigned int \&}]{Sub\+Address}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \texorpdfstring{$\ast$}{*}}]{Data}{, }\item[{const unsigned long \&}]{Data\+Length\+\_\+in\+\_\+bit}{, }\item[{const uint8\+\_\+t \&}]{Start\+Bit}{ = {\ttfamily 0}}\end{DoxyParamCaption})}



This function sets a value for a device on the sequencer. What this means depends on the device type. This function gives us an easy way to add new functionality to a device without having to programm new DLL functions. In contrast to Set\+Register, Set\+Value can execute more complex operations, such as calculating a DDS frequency tuning word from the given frequency and then programming that. There can be several Set\+Value functions for the same Set\+Register function. There can be Set\+Value functions that set some parameter, or that trigger some action, not even necessarily related to the registers of the device. 


\begin{DoxyParams}{Parameters}
{\em Sequencer} & the sequencer to use. \\
\hline
{\em Address} & the address of the device to set the value for. \\
\hline
{\em Sub\+Address} & the subaddress of the device to set the value for. \\
\hline
{\em Data} & the data to set. \\
\hline
{\em Data\+Length\+\_\+in\+\_\+bit} & the length of the data in bits. \\
\hline
{\em Start\+Bit} & the start bit of the data to set. \\
\hline
\end{DoxyParams}
\begin{DoxyReturn}{Returns}
See return convention above. 
\end{DoxyReturn}

Transmits only changes of sequence over TCP/\+IP to sequencer in order to safe time. Once a sequence is in the sequencer\textquotesingle{}s memory, the next sequence is compared to the one in memory and only the changes are transmitted over TCP/\+IP, if this results in less transmission data.
